Dispatched a fresh-eyes Explore agent in parallel for a copy/accessibility/broken-thing re-sweep (given the full accumulated already-swept list) but did not wait on it before landing this independently-verified fix, same precedent as the 2026-09-26 backtest-core.js run. With almost every `functions/api/*.js` handler already at or near 100% branch coverage and `research-engine.js` logged 35+ times as too large for one pass with shrinking per-run returns, looked instead at `functions/api/_lib/session.js` itself — imported by verify-premium, logout-premium, research-engine, assistant, auth-google, and the course-page middleware, but never itself the subject of a coverage-focused run; `loadEntitlementByRef`/`loadEntitlementByCodeHash` in particular had never been unit-tested at all, only exercised indirectly through well-formed refs. lcov BRDA diffing found 8 zero-hit branches: `verifySessionToken`'s `typeof payload !== 'object'` guard (every test signs a real object payload); `readSessionCookie`'s `idx === -1` skip for a Cookie-header piece with no `=`; `entitlementRowState`'s `session_epoch` default-to-1 ternary (every fixture sets it explicitly); both lookup functions' `|| ''` fallback and regex-reject branches; and `sessionEntitlementCheck`'s `!session || typeof session !== 'object'` guard. Added 6 new test cases (2 extend existing tests in place, 4 are new). Sabotage-verified all 8 individually, one at a time, restoring `functions/api/_lib/session.js` byte-for-byte after each (confirmed via `diff` against a pre-session backup, clean throughout). 2 turned out behaviorally redundant for every reachable fixture and are logged honestly rather than claimed as crash-preventing: removing the cookie-piece `=`-guard still fails the subsequent name-equality check (a piece with no `=` never happens to slice into `__Host-vjm_session`), and removing the payload-typeof guard still gets caught by the downstream `Number.isFinite(Number(payload.exp))` check, because JS's permissive property access on any primitive (`(42).exp`, `"str".exp`, `true.exp`) always reads back `undefined` regardless of type — same class as the auth-google.js/market-brief.js precedent for a real, correct, but not-independently-load-bearing branch. The other 6 are genuinely load-bearing, and one is a real fail-open bug the guard alone prevents: without `sessionEntitlementCheck`'s non-object-session check, a non-object `session` (e.g. a bare string, which could only reach this function via a future caller bug, not today's `getSession()` path — `verifySessionToken` already guarantees an object) reads `session.mr` as `undefined`, `loadEntitlementByRef(env, undefined)` returns `{ok:false, reason:'unusable_ref'}`, and `sessionEntitlementCheck`'s own soft-fail-open default (`return { ok: !strict, reason: lookup.reason }` for `!lookup.ok`) converts that failed lookup into **granted access** — confirmed by sabotaging the guard and watching a bare-string session pass with `ok: true`. Also confirmed the epoch-default ternary is load-bearing: without it, a row with a missing/malformed `session_epoch` computes `NaN` instead of `1`, and `NaN > minted` is always `false` in JS, silently defeating the mid-session epoch-bump revocation mechanism (migration 0006's whole reason for existing) for that specific row. And confirmed both lookup functions' regex guards are load-bearing: removing either lets a malformed ref/hash fall through to the `db.prepare()` call instead of failing closed before it, changing the failure's `reason` from `unusable_ref`/`unusable_hash` to `db_error` (proven with a fake D1 binding whose `prepare()` throws "must not be queried for malformed input"). 582/582 tests (+4 new test cases, 2 existing tests extended); `check:syntax` clean (29 files); test-only diff, `functions/api/_lib/session.js` confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged after every sabotage/restore cycle; no doc byte-count pin references this file (not an HTML page); commit pushed to origin/pj — claude (autonomous maintenance run)

The site's existing contrast audit (color-audit.mjs/contrast-audit.mjs, and tests/palette.test.mjs's WCAG checks) was scoped to tokens.css-derived pairs; assets/live-ticker.js injects its own independent hardcoded `<style>` block, so its four `.lt-pct` color states were never in scope, and the existing palette.test.mjs WCAG test for this widget only pins light-mode `.lt-pct.down`/`.lt-sess.sess-cl`, not the other two. Computed contrast via the standard WCAG relative-luminance formula (`.7rem`/11.2px bold text, still under the 18.66px-bold "large text" threshold, so 4.5:1 applies): dark-mode (default) `.lt-pct.down{color:#d14343}` measured 4.28:1 against `#ticker-wrap`'s own hardcoded `#0c0c0d` background; light-mode `.lt-pct.up{color:#1a8a45}` measured 4.08:1 against `--bg2` (`#f6f6f7`, the light-mode ticker background per site.css:1146, `body.light-mode #ticker-wrap{background:var(--bg2)}`) — both fail AA. The dark-mode/dark-sibling pair (`#3ecf72` at 9.66:1) and light-mode down (`#b3251d` at 6.56:1) both pass comfortably, so this was two specific literals, not a wholesale palette problem. Strong corroborating precedent for the dark-mode fix: assets/newsletter.css:17 already documents fixing this *exact* `#d14343`-on-dark-card contrast failure by swapping to `--vjm-red-bright` ("`#d14343` measures 3.95:1... under the 4.5 minimum... the bright token clears it"), a fix that was never propagated to live-ticker.js's own independent copy of the same literal. Fixed dark-mode `.lt-pct.down` to `#e26060` (`--vjm-red-bright`, tokens.css:15) — now 5.66:1. tokens.css has no equivalent bright-green token for the light-mode "up" state (`--vjm-green` there is a neutral gray unrelated to the ticker's standalone green literals, which the palette test's own `TICKER_UP_GREEN` allowlist already documents as a deliberate one-off owner decision, 2026-09-02, scoped to exactly this file), so darkened `#1a8a45`→`#157a3a` by hand, verified at 5.02:1 against `--bg2` — comfortably clears AA while staying recognizably the same green. Updated `TICKER_UP_GREEN`'s pinned light-mode literal in tests/palette.test.mjs to match the new value (otherwise the "no foreign hues" test would flag the new literal as unlisted), and added a new test, `ticker up/down percentages clear WCAG AA against their own theme background, both themes`, extracting both theme backgrounds from site.css itself (not hardcoded) and pinning both newly-fixed ratios. Verified load-bearing: reverted just the two color literals in assets/live-ticker.js to their pre-fix values, re-ran tests/palette.test.mjs, watched both the new contrast assertion (`dark-mode .lt-pct.down (#d14343)... is 4.28:1`) and the pre-existing "no foreign hues" test fail (the reverted `#1a8a45` no longer matched the updated allowlist), then restored the fix and confirmed the full suite green. 583/583 tests (+1 new test); `check:syntax` clean (29 files); color-audit.mjs re-run clean (site-wide red share unchanged at 20.3%, non-red chromatic hues still exactly 2 — the same allowlisted ticker greens, just retuned, not a new stray hue). assets/live-ticker.js renders on index.html and premarket.html, both public, unauthenticated pages — no route/auth/backend changes, no doc byte-count pin references this file (a JS asset, not an HTML page); commit pushed to origin/pj — claude (autonomous maintenance run)

Of the 5 zero-hit branches: line 107's `if (!code)` is already exercised by tests/whop-webhook-code-generation-failure.test.mjs's `mock.module`-based test (the code-generation-exhaustion rollback) but never merges into this file's lcov record — confirmed by running that test file's coverage in isolation, which enumerates a completely different, shorter branch set (23 vs 67) for the same source file; `mock.module`'s fresh dynamic import creates a separate V8 script instance whose branch ids don't merge with the normally-imported instance's, so this is a tooling artifact, not a real gap (same class as the indexing.js/research-engine.js `NODE_V8_COVERAGE` overlap notes above). The other 4 were real: `normalizeWhopEvent` (integrations-core.js) can legitimately return a null `memberId` or `productId` when Whop's payload omits `user_id`/`product_id` entirely, and every `grantBody()` call in the whole test file supplies both — so the grant handler's `evt.memberId || 'unknown'`/`evt.productId || 'unknown'` Discord-embed fallbacks and its `evt.memberId || '?'` audit-note fallback had never fired; without them the message an owner reads to hand a customer their code would show a literal "Member: `null`", and the audit note would read "grant:complete:null:delivered". The 5th, a revoke for a member id with zero matching code rows (`res.meta.changes` staying 0) had likewise never been tested — every revoke test on record first granted the same member a code. Added 4 cases to tests/whop-webhook-api.test.mjs's existing per-scenario-block style: two capturing the Discord embed body via a fetch mock and asserting the "unknown" placeholders for a missing member id and a missing product id respectively; one asserting the audit note reads `grant:complete:?:delivered` for the missing-member-id case; one asserting a revoke of a member with no code at all returns `revoked: 0` rather than throwing. Sabotage-verified 3 of the 4: removed each `|| 'unknown'`/`|| '?'` fallback in turn in `functions/api/whop-webhook.js`, watched the corresponding assertion fail with the literal `null` string, restored byte-for-byte (`diff` against a pre-edit backup clean) before landing. Could not sabotage-verify the 4th (`revoked: 0`) the same way: the test file's fake D1 always returns a well-formed `{meta:{changes:0}}`, so `res.meta.changes` already evaluates to the falsy value `0`, not `undefined` — `0 || 0` and a bare `0` are identical, so removing `res.meta.changes) || 0`'s fallback doesn't change this assertion's outcome. Logging this honestly rather than claiming it as crash-preventing: the fallback only matters if `res` or `res.meta` were ever absent, which Cloudflare D1's `.run()` contract doesn't allow in practice — a real, correct, but not independently load-bearing branch (same class as the auth-google.js/session.js `|| ''` precedents above), while the test itself still pins a genuine, previously-unproven behavior (an unknown-member revoke must not throw). `whop-webhook.js` branch coverage 92.54%→98.53% (67/68; the one remaining zero-hit branch is the documented mock.module merge artifact, already covered by its own dedicated test file). 584/584 tests (count unchanged — new assertions live inside the existing top-level test file); `check:syntax` clean (29 files); test-only diff, `functions/api/whop-webhook.js` confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged after every sabotage/restore cycle; no doc byte-count pins reference this file (not a page); commit pushed to origin/pj — claude (autonomous maintenance run)

- 2026-09-27 — closed one real branch-coverage gap in `functions/api/assistant.js`'s POST handler (92.45%→93.46%) in tests/assistant-api.test.mjs. Read AGENTS.md → board.md → decisions.md tail first this run; hit the standing disjoint-shallow-clone anomaly again on arrival (local `pj` had no merge-base against `origin/pj`) — working tree clean, `git reset --hard origin/pj` landed on tip `22dcdef`. 584/584 tests green going in. Live-deployment probe: direct curl CONNECT to `pj.vjm.pages.dev` returned `403` on the homepage, all four required JSON endpoints, and the verify-premium bad-code POST, corroborated by the agent-proxy `status` endpoint's fresh `connect_rejected` entries — the same standing whole-`*.pages.dev`-domain policy block every prior run has hit; per the standing 2026-09-19/20 decision did not attempt the banned Actions-workflow probe-borrow. `handle()`'s `try { body = await request.json(); } catch { return json({ ok: false, error: 'Invalid request.' }, 400); }` had never actually caught anything: every test builds its request body through the file's `ask()` helper, which runs the body through `JSON.stringify()` before sending, so the body is always syntactically valid JSON even in the already-tested literal-`null` case. A body truncated in transit, or any other genuinely unparseable JSON text, is a real thing a client can send, and this inner catch is what keeps that answering with a clean 400 instead of falling through to `onRequestPost`'s outer catch-all and its misleading 502 ('Assistant is temporarily unavailable.') — the exact same misdiagnosis-as-server-outage defect class the 2026-09-15 entry above fixed for a `null` body in this same file. Added one test sending a raw `'{not valid json'` string body directly through `onRequestPost()` (bypassing `ask()`'s stringify), with a `fetch` guard proving Alpaca is never reached, asserting 400/`ok:false`. - 2026-09-27 — fixed a real fail-open-then-lies-the-other-way bug in the code sign-in path: Whop's `payment.succeeded` webhook event fires on every RENEWAL charge, not just the initial purchase (see WHOP_GRANT_EVENTS in `_lib/integrations-core.js`), and hits the same 'grant' branch in `whop-webhook.js` as a first purchase, which always mints a brand-new access code and INSERTs a brand-new `whop_codes` row rather than extending the row the member already holds. The new code is delivered only to the owner's private Discord channel, never to the customer, so the member keeps using their original code — and once that specific row's own `expires_at` (computed from their first billing period) passes, `verify-premium.js`'s exact-code-hash lookup finds it `expired` and returns `MEMBERSHIP_ENDED` ("Renew on Whop...") to a real, currently-paying customer. `auth-google.js` already handles this exact multi-row-per-renewal reality — it selects every row for the account's `email_hash` and grants on any live one — but the code-sign-in path never got the equivalent treatment, since a typed code only ever looked up the single row matching its own hash. Found by a dedicated fresh-eyes Explore agent explicitly told not to report a bare coverage percentage but to find something with real user/business impact; it traced the whole chain (`integrations-core.js` -> `whop-webhook.js` -> `session.js` -> `verify-premium.js` -> `auth-google.js`) before reporting, and I independently re-verified every file/line it cited before touching anything. Fixed by adding `session.js`'s `loadLiveEntitlementForMember(env, whopMemberId)`, mirroring `auth-google.js`'s multi-row lookup but keyed on `whop_member_id` (a code has no email on hand); it fails closed to `null` on any DB error or missing id, so it can only ever grant access it can independently prove is live — it never introduces a new fail-open path, same safety shape as every other D1-lookup helper in this file. Added `whop_member_id` to the shared `ENTITLEMENT_COLUMNS` select (additive; `loadEntitlementByRef`'s existing callers are unaffected by an extra column). Wired `verify-premium.js`'s D1 branch: when the exact-code row isn't live, check for a live sibling row for the same `whop_member_id` before declaring the membership ended, and if found, mint the session from THAT row (its own `code_hash` re-derives the correct `member_ref` for future `loadEntitlementByRef` lookups, not the originally-typed code's hash). Deliberately did NOT touch `whop-webhook.js`'s grant/provisioning logic — always minting a fresh code per renewal and delivering only to Discord is still the existing (separately fixable) design; this fix closes the read-side gap that was actually causing paying members to be told their membership had ended. Added 3 cases to tests/verify-premium-api.test.mjs: a live sibling row grants access (asserting the response/session reflect the WINNING row, not the expired one); no live sibling still refuses (every other row for the member also dead); a row with no `whop_member_id` at all doesn't throw trying to look up siblings. Extended the file's `makeDb` fake to answer `.all()` (the new sibling query) alongside its existing `.first()` (the exact-code query), matching auth-google-api.test.mjs's own `.all()`-based fake shape. Verified load-bearing: forced the new sibling-lookup branch off (`if (false && !state.live && ...)`), ran just this test file, watched the new "live sibling row must grant access" case fail (403 instead of 200), restored `functions/api/verify-premium.js` byte-for-byte (`diff` clean) before landing. 584/584 tests (count unchanged — new cases live inside the existing top-level test file); `check:syntax` clean (29 files); no docs/PAYWALL.md or docs/MASTER-AUDIT.md byte-count pins reference these files (neither is a page); docs/API.md's `/api/verify-premium` response-shape contract is unchanged (still 200/403/401/429/503, just a previously-mislabeled 403 case now correctly resolves to 200). Live-deployment probe: this sandbox's egress to `pj.vjm.pages.dev` remains 403'd (curl CONNECT tunnel 403; agent-proxy `status`'s `recentRelayFailures` confirmed a fresh `connect_rejected` for this exact run) — the same standing whole-`*.pages.dev`-domain policy block every prior run has hit; per the standing 2026-09-20 decision did not attempt the banned Actions-workflow probe-borrow; citing run 35511305220 (2026-09-20) for last-known-good live status, since this run's own 584/584 pass over the sign-in path it touches is the stronger evidence for this specific diff. - 2026-09-30 — closed `functions/api/_lib/backtest-core.js`'s `nonOverlapping` event-dedup coverage gap (Edge Lab's `runEventStudy`, lines 418/422/438): the only reachable, config-driven branch in the file with zero test coverage and zero live callers (Edge Lab isn't wired to a route yet — confirmed via a repo-wide `grep -rn nonOverlap`, read only inside this file). Matters because every statistic this engine reports (win rate, its Wilson-score interval, avg/median R-multiple) assumes each collected event is an independent trade; without a verified dedup path, a trigger re-firing on consecutive bars while the first trade is still open would let one underlying price swing get counted as several overlapping "trades," inflating sample size and every rate built on it — the same overstated-backtest class this file's own `TIMING_CONVENTION`/hypothetical-results framing and its 2026-08-31/09-26 MFE/MAE and streak-reversal sign-bug fixes exist to prevent.
